Validate, read-only (do not modify files), at your most careful. The question: is this post a FAIR, like-for-like comparison of two devices, or does it favour one?

The post (site/page.tsx, cover in site/studies-entry.ts) compares a Galaxy Tab S10+ (MediaTek Dimensity 9300+) and a Galaxy S24 Ultra (Snapdragon 8 Gen 3) on prefill/decode processor pairings (CPU, GPU, NPU for reading the prompt, then for writing the reply). Raw data: mediatek/s4 (Llama 3.2 1B), s5 (Qwen3 0.6B, s5 + s5b), s6 (Gemma 3 1B); snapdragon/s24a (Qwen3 0.6B complete; a partial Llama that must not be used), s24b (Llama 3.2 1B, then Gemma 3 1B on a six-conversation subset; IN PROGRESS, judge the generator's handling, not the missing numbers). Benches: pd_bench_mtk.cpp (tablet), pd_bench_sd.cpp (S24). Generator: site/build-prefill-decode-mtk-data.mjs -> site/prefill-decode-mtk-data.ts (sdModels = S24 rows; sdTwins = the tablet's rows rebuilt on exactly the S24's turns). NOTES.md for both.

Check, with file:line and the numbers:
1. Symmetry: are both devices measured, computed and presented the same way (same turns and work per turn, same statistics, same colours/order/labels, same denominators)? Any asymmetric framing, headline, ordering, or wording that favours one? Is the cover's hook (median NPU/CPU prefill ratio per device) fair?
2. Differences that are real and must be disclosed: NPU runtimes (MediaTek's runtime with its own A16W8 export vs llama.cpp Hexagon on Q4_0), GPU APIs (Vulkan vs OpenCL), tablet vs phone thermal envelope, CPU core mix, process splits/order on the tablet, units, heat-reading cadence, the gate outcomes. Are all disclosed where they matter, and is any claim stronger than one unit per model per device and one run per pairing supports?
3. The heat comparison: both from each device's own logger over each model's turns. Are the logger formats parsed the same way (the S24 has four CPU clusters, the tablet three)? Is "top" defined the same way? Is "neither got stuck" supported?
4. The "finished first" finding: each device's own fastest pairing; is the causal-sounding juxtaposition with core counts acceptable or misleading?
5. The tablet-only section in the fold: clearly separated and not used in any comparison?
6. Anything in the generator that could mix devices, mismatch turns, or publish an incomplete S24 model.
Rank by severity; state plainly where you find nothing. Your answer is what you print after exploring.

     1	#!/usr/bin/env node
     2	// Generates lib/prefill-decode-mtk-data.ts from OpenWeights' raw MediaTek results
     3	// (tools/mediatek/results/2026-11-16-mt6989/s*/results-*.jsonl), so the MediaTek prefill/decode
     4	// page never retypes a number.
     5	//
     6	//   OPENWEIGHTS=../../mobile-inference node scripts/build-prefill-decode-mtk-data.mjs [ref|worktree]
     7	//
     8	// Each model's nine cells ran on one Galaxy Tab S10+ unit in one Device Streaming session, after a
     9	// throwaway warm-up, each cell behind a cooling gate (AP and SKIN at or under 55 C, every CPU
    10	// ceiling at its top, thermal status 0, at most 120 s). This tablet passed the gate before almost
    11	// every cell, so unlike the Snapdragon page no cell is set aside for starting cool: all compared.
    12	import { execFileSync } from "node:child_process";
    13	import { readFileSync, writeFileSync } from "node:fs";
    14	import { resolve } from "node:path";
    15	
    16	const OPENWEIGHTS = resolve(process.env.OPENWEIGHTS ?? "../../mobile-inference");
    17	const REF = process.argv[2] ?? "worktree";
    18	const DIR = "tools/mediatek/results/2026-11-16-mt6989";
    19	const WORKTREE = REF === "worktree";
    20	const commit = WORKTREE ? "worktree" : execFileSync("git", ["-C", OPENWEIGHTS, "rev-parse", REF], { encoding: "utf8" }).trim();
    21	
    22	// A JSONL file under a results folder of the OpenWeights tree (the MediaTek one unless given).
    23	const readIn = (dir) => (path) =>
    24	  (WORKTREE
    25	    ? readFileSync(resolve(OPENWEIGHTS, dir, path), "utf8")
    26	    : execFileSync("git", ["-C", OPENWEIGHTS, "show", `${commit}:${dir}/${path}`], { encoding: "utf8", maxBuffer: 1 << 30 })
    27	  )
    28	    .split("\n")
    29	    .filter((l) => l.startsWith("{"))
    30	    .map((l) => JSON.parse(l));
    31	const read = readIn(DIR);
    32	// The Galaxy S24 Ultra (Snapdragon 8 Gen 3) run, for the same-generation comparison.
    33	const SD_DIR = "tools/snapdragon/results/2026-11-16-s24";
    34	
    35	// One entry per model: the files that hold its cells (one session, one unit), and the NPU export it
    36	// used. Units are the first 8 characters of sha256(ro.serialno). From Qwen3 1.7B up the NPU export and
    37	// a llama.cpp copy of the model do not fit in the tablet's memory together (NOTES.md, session 8), so
    38	// those models ran only the five pairings without a handoff (fits: 5); the other four are null.
    39	const models = [
    40	  { file: "Llama-3.2-1B-Instruct-Q4_0", name: "Llama 3.2 1B", quant: "Q4_0", npu: "A16W8", files: ["s4/results-s4.jsonl"], unit: "4bff7833", session: "4" },
    41	  { file: "Qwen3-0.6B-Q4_0", name: "Qwen3 0.6B", quant: "Q4_0", npu: "A16W8", files: ["s5/results-s5.jsonl", "s5/results-s5b.jsonl"], unit: "d6761b43", session: "5" },
    42	  { file: "google_gemma-3-1b-it-Q4_0", name: "Gemma 3 1B", quant: "Q4_0", npu: "A16W8", files: ["s6/results-s6.jsonl"], unit: "317808dd", session: "6" },
    43	  { file: "LFM2.5-1.2B-Instruct-QAD-Q4_0", name: "LFM2.5 1.2B", quant: "Q4_0, hybrid", npu: "A16W8", files: ["s7/results-s7.jsonl"], unit: "d6761b43", session: "7" },
    44	  { file: "Qwen3-1.7B-Q4_0", name: "Qwen3 1.7B", quant: "Q4_0", npu: "A16W4", files: ["s8/results-s8.jsonl"], unit: "c8340578", session: "8", fits: 5 },
    45	  { file: "Qwen3-1.7B-Q8_0", name: "Qwen3 1.7B", quant: "Q8_0", npu: "A16W4", files: ["s8/results-s8.jsonl"], unit: "c8340578", session: "8", fits: 5 },
    46	  // Nine pairings over every turn would not finish in one 3-hour session, so this model read two
    47	  // conversations of each dataset (phone/conversations-6.jsonl, the first two of each in file order).
    48	  { file: "LFM2.5-2.6B-QAD-Q4_0", name: "LFM2.5 2.6B", quant: "Q4_0, hybrid", npu: "A16W4", files: ["s9/results-s9.jsonl"], unit: "e880d908", session: "9", convs: 6 },
    49	  { file: "Llama-3.2-3B-Instruct-Q4_0", name: "Llama 3.2 3B", quant: "Q4_0", npu: "A16W4", files: ["s11/results-s11.jsonl"], unit: "4e2d9065", session: "11", fits: 5 },
    50	  // Its NPU export did not load at all (fits: 4): only the llama.cpp pairings.
    51	  { file: "Qwen3-4B-Q4_0", name: "Qwen3 4B", quant: "Q4_0", npu: "A16W4", files: ["s10/results-s10.jsonl"], unit: "4bff7833", session: "10", fits: 4 },
    52	];
    53	const P = ["cpu", "gpu", "npu"];
    54	const HANDOFF = new Set(["cpu:npu", "npu:cpu", "gpu:npu", "npu:gpu"]);
    55	const STALL_MS = 500;
    56	const round = (x, d) => Math.round(x * 10 ** d) / 10 ** d;
    57	const sum = (xs) => xs.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0);
    58	const median = (xs) => {
    59	  const s = [...xs].sort((a, b) => a - b);
    60	  return s.length % 2 ? s[(s.length - 1) / 2] : (s[s.length / 2 - 1] + s[s.length / 2]) / 2;
    61	};
    62	const base = (m) =>
    63	  m
    64	    .split("/")
    65	    .pop()
    66	    .replace(/\.gguf$/, "");
    67	
    68	// The Snapdragon's NPU prefill against its CPU prefill on the same turns this tablet ran, from the
    69	// Snapdragon page's own run for the model (its data module names it) and that run's raw rows, pooled
    70	// over the cells the Snapdragon page compares (not its cool starts). The tablet reads only the turns
    71	// that fit the NPU's 2,048-token window, and prefill rates change with prompt length, so the two
    72	// chips' ratios are only comparable on matched turns (Codex review, round four).
    73	const pd = readFileSync(resolve(import.meta.dirname, "../lib/prefill-decode-data.ts"), "utf8");
    74	const pdStart = pd.indexOf("= [", pd.indexOf("export const pdModels")) + 2;
    75	const pdModels = JSON.parse(pd.slice(pdStart, pd.indexOf("];", pdStart) + 1));
    76	const snapdragonRatio = (m, common) => {
    77	  const twin = pdModels.find((s) => s.name === m.name && s.quant === m.quant);
    78	  if (!twin) return null;
    79	  let rows;
    80	  try {
    81	    rows = readFileSync(resolve(OPENWEIGHTS, `tools/snapdragon/results/2026-10-24-sm8850/results-${twin.run}.jsonl`), "utf8")
    82	      .split("\n")
    83	      .filter((l) => l.startsWith("{"))
    84	      .map((l) => JSON.parse(l));
    85	  } catch {
    86	    return null;
    87	  }
    88	  const ok = (r) => {
    89	    if (r.kind !== "turn" || r.error || r.label !== twin.run || base(r.model) !== m.file || !common.has(`${r.conv}#${r.turn}`)) return false;
    90	    const c = twin.cells[P.indexOf(r.prefill)][P.indexOf(r.decode)];
    91	    return c && !c.coolStart && c.run === twin.run;
    92	  };
    93	  const rate = (p) => {
    94	    const rs = rows.filter((r) => ok(r) && r.prefill === p);
    95	    return rs.length ? sum(rs.map((r) => r.new_tokens)) / sum(rs.map((r) => r.prompt_ms)) : null;
    96	  };
    97	  const npu = rate("npu");
    98	  const cpu = rate("cpu");
    99	  const turnsMatched = new Set(rows.filter(ok).map((r) => `${r.conv}#${r.turn}`)).size;
   100	  return npu && cpu ? { run: twin.run, npuOverCpu: round(npu / cpu, 2), turns: turnsMatched } : null;
   101	};
   102	
   103	// The S24's rows come from the same build so they meet the same checks: all nine pairings in one
   104	// llama.cpp process (CPU, OpenCL, Hexagon), the tablet's turns and seeded orders.
   105	const sdModels = [
   106	  { file: "Qwen3-0.6B-Q4_0", name: "Qwen3 0.6B", quant: "Q4_0", npu: "Hexagon v75", files: ["s24a/results-s24a.jsonl"], dir: SD_DIR, unit: "ccc0f521", session: "s24a" },
   107	  // Llama 3.2 1B and Gemma 3 1B ran in a second session (unit a64940db) after the first one's driver
   108	  // cancelled on an adb gap; the first session's three Llama pairings (s24a) are left out, so each
   109	  // model's cells stay on one unit.
   110	  {
   111	    file: "Llama-3.2-1B-Instruct-Q4_0",
   112	    name: "Llama 3.2 1B",
   113	    quant: "Q4_0",
   114	    npu: "Hexagon v75",
   115	    files: ["s24b/results-s24b.jsonl"],
   116	    dir: SD_DIR,
   117	    unit: "a64940db",
   118	    session: "s24b",
   119	  },
   120	  {
   121	    file: "google_gemma-3-1b-it-Q4_0",
   122	    name: "Gemma 3 1B",
   123	    quant: "Q4_0",
   124	    npu: "Hexagon v75",
   125	    files: ["s24b/results-s24b.jsonl"],
   126	    dir: SD_DIR,
   127	    unit: "a64940db",
   128	    session: "s24b",
   129	    convs: 6,
   130	  },
   131	];
   132	
   133	const build = (list) => {
   134	  const out = [];
   135	  for (const m of list) {
   136	    let files;
   137	    try {
   138	      files = m.files.map(readIn(m.dir ?? DIR));
   139	    } catch {
   140	      continue; // not run yet
   141	    }
   142	    const mine = (r) => r.model && base(r.model) === m.file && !String(r.label).endsWith("warm");
   143	    const rows = files.flatMap((f) => f.filter(mine));
   144	    const turns = rows.filter((r) => r.kind === "turn" && !r.error);
   145	    const cellOf = (r) => `${r.prefill}:${r.decode}`;
   146	    // A cell counts only once the process that ran it finished: a done row for the same model and label
   147	    // after the cell's start row, in the same file. One turn used to be enough, so a session still
   148	    // running reached the page (Codex review, round four).
   149	    const finished = new Set();
   150	    for (const f of files)
   151	      f.forEach((r, i) => {
   152	        if (mine(r) && r.kind === "cell" && r.when === "start" && f.slice(i + 1).some((x) => mine(x) && x.kind === "done" && x.label === r.label)) finished.add(cellOf(r));
   153	      });
   154	    const present = P.flatMap((p) => P.map((d) => `${p}:${d}`)).filter((c) => turns.some((r) => cellOf(r) === c));
   155	    if (present.some((c) => !finished.has(c))) continue; // still running
   156	    // One row per cell and turn, and every cell doing the same work on each turn, or the model is wrong.
   157	    const seen = new Set();
   158	    for (const r of turns) {
   159	      const k = `${cellOf(r)}#${r.conv}#${r.turn}`;
   160	      if (seen.has(k)) throw new Error(`${m.file}: duplicate turn ${k}`);
   161	      seen.add(k);
   162	    }
   163	    const fits = m.fits ?? 9;
   164	    // 9: every pairing; 5: no handoff between the NPU and llama.cpp; 4: no NPU at all.
   165	    if (present.length !== fits || (fits < 9 && present.some((c) => HANDOFF.has(c))) || (fits === 4 && present.includes("npu:npu"))) continue;
   166	    // The turns every cell completed: the comparison set.
   167	    const key = (r) => `${r.conv}#${r.turn}`;
   168	    let common = null;
   169	    for (const c of present) {
   170	      const ks = new Set(turns.filter((r) => cellOf(r) === c).map(key));
   171	      common = common ? new Set([...common].filter((k) => ks.has(k))) : ks;
   172	    }
   173	    // A restricted build keeps only the given turns (the tablet's rows on a subset the S24 read).
   174	    if (m.only) common = new Set([...common].filter((k) => m.only.has(k)));
   175	    if (!common.size) continue;
   176	    for (const k of common) {
   177	      const w = new Set(turns.filter((r) => key(r) === k && present.includes(cellOf(r))).map((r) => `${r.pos}/${r.new_tokens}/${r.decode_steps}`));
   178	      if (w.size !== 1) throw new Error(`${m.file} ${k}: cells did different work ${[...w]}`);
   179	    }
   180	    const gates = Object.fromEntries(rows.filter((r) => r.kind === "cooled").map((r) => [cellOf(r), r]));
   181	    const loads = rows.filter((r) => r.kind === "load");
   182	    const order = [...new Set(turns.map(cellOf))];
   183	    const cells = P.map((p) =>
   184	      P.map((d) => {
   185	        if (!present.includes(`${p}:${d}`)) return null;
   186	        const rs = turns.filter((r) => r.prefill === p && r.decode === d && common.has(key(r)));
   187	        const g = gates[`${p}:${d}`];
   188	        return {
   189	          seconds: round(sum(rs.map((r) => r.turn_ms)) / 1000, 3),
   190	          prefillTokS: round((sum(rs.map((r) => r.new_tokens)) / sum(rs.map((r) => r.prompt_ms))) * 1000, 1),
   191	          decodeTokS: round((sum(rs.map((r) => r.decode_steps)) / sum(rs.map((r) => r.decode_ms))) * 1000, 2),
   192	          handoffSeconds: round(sum(rs.map((r) => r.xfer_get_ms + r.xfer_set_ms)) / 1000, 3),
   193	          decodeShare: round(sum(rs.map((r) => r.decode_ms)) / sum(rs.map((r) => r.turn_ms)), 5),
   194	          stalls: d === "cpu" ? sum(rs.map((r) => r.step_ms.filter((s) => s > STALL_MS).length)) : 0,
   195	          longestStepS: round(Math.max(0, ...rs.flatMap((r) => r.step_ms)) / 1000, 3),
   196	          startApC: round(median(rs.map((r) => r.before_ap_c)), 1),
   197	          cappedTurns: rs.filter((r) => r.before_cpu_capped).length,
   198	          hotTurns: rs.filter((r) => (r.before_status ?? 0) >= 3).length,
   199	          gate: g?.gate ?? "?",
   200	          gateWaitS: g?.waited_s ?? null,
   201	          order: order.indexOf(`${p}:${d}`) + 1,
   202	        };
   203	      }),
   204	    );
   205	    const pooled = (phase) =>
   206	      P.map((x) => {
   207	        const rs = turns.filter((r) => r[phase] === x && common.has(key(r)));
   208	        if (!rs.length) return null;
   209	        return phase === "prefill"
   210	          ? { tokS: round((sum(rs.map((r) => r.new_tokens)) / sum(rs.map((r) => r.prompt_ms))) * 1000, 2), firstTokenMs: round(median(rs.map((r) => r.prefill_ms)), 1) }
   211	          : { tokS: round((sum(rs.map((r) => r.decode_steps)) / sum(rs.map((r) => r.decode_ms))) * 1000, 3) };
   212	      });
   213	    const npuLoad = loads.filter((r) => r.proc === "npu" && !r.error);
   214	    // Heat while this model ran, from its own device's logger (mtklog on the tablet, sdlog on the S24,
   215	    // the same columns named in a header line), limited to this model's compared turns so both devices
   216	    // are read over the same work. Each CPU policy's top comes from the turn rows ("policy:limit/top").
   217	    const heat = (() => {
   218	      const folder = m.files[0].split("/")[0];
   219	      const text = [`${folder}/mtklog-${folder}.txt`, `${folder}/sdlog-${folder}.txt`]
   220	        .map((f) => {
   221	          try {
   222	            return WORKTREE
   223	              ? readFileSync(resolve(OPENWEIGHTS, m.dir ?? DIR, f), "utf8")
   224	              : execFileSync("git", ["-C", OPENWEIGHTS, "show", `${commit}:${m.dir ?? DIR}/${f}`], { encoding: "utf8", maxBuffer: 1 << 30 });
   225	          } catch {
   226	            return null;
   227	          }
   228	        })
   229	        .find(Boolean);
   230	      if (!text) return null;
   231	      const ls = text.split("\n");
   232	      const head = (ls.find((l) => l.startsWith("epoch")) ?? "").split("|")[0].trim().split(/\s+/);
   233	      const pols = head.flatMap((h, i) => {
   234	        const mm = /^p(\d+)_max$/.exec(h);
   235	        return mm ? [{ p: mm[1], i }] : [];
   236	      });
   237	      const mt = turns.filter((r) => common.has(key(r)));
   238	      const t0 = Math.min(...mt.map((r) => r.wall_ms)) / 1000;
   239	      const t1 = Math.max(...mt.map((r) => r.wall_ms + r.turn_ms)) / 1000;
   240	      const tops = Object.fromEntries(
   241	        String(mt[0].before_cpu_max_mhz)
   242	          .split(",")
   243	          .map((x) => [x.split(":")[0], Number(x.split(":")[1].split("/")[1]) * 1000]),
   244	      );
   245	      const rowsIn = ls.filter((l) => /^\d/.test(l) && Number(l.split(" ")[0]) >= t0 && Number(l.split(" ")[0]) <= t1);
   246	      const f = rowsIn.map((l) => l.split("|")[0].trim().split(/\s+/));
   247	      const hal = rowsIn.filter((l) => l.includes("|")).map((l) => l.split("|")[1].trim().split(/\s+/));
   248	      if (!f.length || !pols.length) return null;
   249	      const atTop = f.filter((x) => pols.every(({ p, i }) => Number(x[i]) >= (tops[p] ?? Infinity) - 1000)).length;
   250	      const steps = mt.filter((r) => r.decode === "cpu").flatMap((r) => r.step_ms);
   251	      const aps = hal.map((h) => Number(h[1])).filter((x) => !Number.isNaN(x));
   252	      return {
   253	        samples: f.length,
   254	        topShare: round((100 * atTop) / f.length, 1),
   255	        lowestGhz: pols.map(({ i }) => round(Math.min(...f.map((x) => Number(x[i]))) / 1e6, 2)),
   256	        criticalShare: round((100 * hal.filter((h) => Number(h[0]) >= 4).length) / Math.max(1, hal.length), 1),
   257	        maxApC: aps.length ? Math.max(...aps) : null,
   258	        allOnline: f.every((x) => x[1] === "0-7"),
   259	        cpuSteps: steps.length,
   260	        slowSteps: steps.filter((x) => x > STALL_MS).length,
   261	      };
   262	    })();
   263	    out.push({
   264	      name: m.name,
   265	      quant: m.quant,
   266	      npuExport: m.npu,
   267	      unit: m.unit,
   268	      session: m.session,
   269	      fits,
   270	      convs: m.convs ?? 9,
   271	      turns: common.size,
   272	      newTokens: sum(turns.filter((r) => cellOf(r) === "cpu:cpu" && common.has(key(r))).map((r) => r.new_tokens)),
   273	      replyTokens: sum(turns.filter((r) => cellOf(r) === "cpu:cpu" && common.has(key(r))).map((r) => r.decode_steps + 1)),
   274	      cpuStepMs: round(median(turns.filter((r) => r.decode === "cpu" && common.has(key(r))).flatMap((r) => r.step_ms)), 1),
   275	      npuMemoryMb: npuLoad.length ? Math.round(median(npuLoad.map((r) => r.avail_before_mb - r.avail_after_mb))) : null,
   276	      cells,
   277	      prefill: pooled("prefill"),
   278	      decode: pooled("decode"),
   279	      snapdragon: snapdragonRatio(m, common),
   280	      // How often the first reply token after an NPU prefill was the CPU's (cpu:cpu's) on the same turn.
   281	      npuFirstToken: (() => {
   282	        const cpu = new Map(turns.filter((r) => cellOf(r) === "cpu:cpu" && common.has(key(r))).map((r) => [key(r), r.tokens[0]]));
   283	        const npu = turns.filter((r) => r.prefill === "npu" && common.has(key(r)) && cpu.has(key(r)));
   284	        return npu.length ? { same: npu.filter((r) => r.tokens[0] === cpu.get(key(r))).length, of: npu.length, tokens: [...new Set(npu.map((r) => r.tokens[0]))].length } : null;
   285	      })(),
   286	      // The turns read, as conversation#turn, so another chip's rows can be checked against them.
   287	      turnKeys: [...common].sort(),
   288	      heat,
   289	    });
   290	  }
   291	  return out;
   292	};
   293	const out = build(models);
   294	const sdOut = build(sdModels);
   295	// The tablet's side of each S24 comparison, on the S24's turns. Where the S24 read every turn the
   296	// tablet did, that is the tablet's own row; where it read a subset (Gemma 3 1B, the six-conversation
   297	// file, so it fit the session), the tablet's rows are rebuilt on those turns alone. Turns the S24 read
   298	// and the tablet did not would make the comparison wrong, so they stop the build.
   299	const sdTwins = sdOut.map((s) => {
   300	  const t = out.find((m) => m.name === s.name && m.quant === s.quant);
   301	  if (!t) return null;
   302	  const extra = s.turnKeys.filter((k) => !t.turnKeys.includes(k));
   303	  if (extra.length) throw new Error(`${s.name}: the S24 read turns the tablet did not: ${extra.slice(0, 3)}`);
   304	  if (t.turnKeys.join() === s.turnKeys.join()) return t;
   305	  const entry = models.find((m) => m.name === s.name && m.quant === s.quant);
   306	  return build([{ ...entry, only: new Set(s.turnKeys) }])[0] ?? null;
   307	});
   308	
   309	// Heat. The probes (phone/hot.sh): one conversation's first three turns on the CPU, 6 and 2 threads,
   310	// cool behind the gate and then straight after 8 shell busy loops and 8 dd loops; and every session's
   311	// logger (phone/mtklog.sh: online CPUs, each CPU policy's ceiling every 2 s; Android's thermal status,
   312	// AP and SKIN every 10 s), with every CPU decode step of that session's matrix.
   313	const lines = (path) => {
   314	  try {
   315	    return (
   316	      WORKTREE
   317	        ? readFileSync(resolve(OPENWEIGHTS, DIR, path), "utf8")
   318	        : execFileSync("git", ["-C", OPENWEIGHTS, "show", `${commit}:${DIR}/${path}`], { encoding: "utf8", maxBuffer: 1 << 30 })
   319	    ).split("\n");
   320	  } catch {
   321	    return null;

   324	const TOP = [2000000, 2850000, 3400000];
   325	const mtkHot = [];
   326	for (const [session, unit] of [
   327	  ["s6", "317808dd"],
   328	  ["s7", "d6761b43"],
   329	]) {
   330	  let rows;
   331	  try {
   332	    rows = read(`${session}/results-${session}hot.jsonl`);
   333	  } catch {
   334	    continue;
   335	  }
   336	  const probes = {};
   337	  const turnsOf = (tag) => rows.filter((r) => r.kind === "turn" && !r.error && r.label.endsWith(`-${tag}`)).sort((x, y) => x.turn - y.turn);
   338	  const keys = (T) => T.map((r) => `${r.conv}#${r.turn}`).join(",");
   339	  for (const tag of ["cool6", "cool2", "hot6", "hot2"]) {
   340	    const T = turnsOf(tag);
   341	    // A pair is kept only when cool and hot both read the same three turns.
   342	    const twin = turnsOf(tag.startsWith("cool") ? `hot${tag.slice(-1)}` : `cool${tag.slice(-1)}`);
   343	    if (T.length !== 3 || keys(T) !== keys(twin)) continue;
   344	    const steps = T.flatMap((r) => r.step_ms);
   345	    probes[tag] = {
   346	      status: T[0].before_status,
   347	      apC: T[0].before_ap_c,
   348	      ceilingsGhz: T[0].before_cpu_max_mhz.split(",").map((x) => Number(x.split(":")[1].split("/")[0]) / 1000),

   350	      decodeTokS: round((sum(T.map((r) => r.decode_steps)) / sum(T.map((r) => r.decode_ms))) * 1000, 2),
   351	      medianStepMs: round(median(steps), 1),
   352	      longestStepMs: Math.round(Math.max(...steps)),
   353	      slowSteps: steps.filter((x) => x > STALL_MS).length,
   354	    };
   355	  }
   356	  // The heat window: from the first heat to the last probe, read from hot.sh's own log.
   357	  const hl = lines(`${session}/hot-${session}hot.log`) ?? [];

   360	  const end = hl.filter((l) => l.includes("probe hot2")).map((l) => Number(l.split(" ")[0]));
   361	  const log = (lines(`${session}/mtklog-${session}.txt`) ?? []).filter((l) => /^\d/.test(l));
   362	  const hal = log
   363	    .map((l) => [Number(l.split(" ")[0]), (l.split("|")[1] ?? "").trim().split(/\s+/)])
   364	    .filter(([t, h]) => on.length && t >= on[0] && t <= (end[0] ?? Infinity) && h.length >= 3);
   365	  mtkHot.push({
   366	    unit,
   367	    session: session.slice(1),
   368	    heatMinutes: on.length ? round((Number(hl.find((l) => l.includes("heat off")).split(" ")[0]) - on[0]) / 60, 1) : null,
   369	    maxStatus: Math.max(...hal.map(([, h]) => Number(h[0]))),
   370	    maxApC: Math.max(...hal.map(([, h]) => Number(h[1]))),
   371	    maxSkinC: Math.max(...hal.map(([, h]) => Number(h[2]))),
   372	    probes,
   373	  });

   390	  const raw = (lines(`${session}/mtklog-${session}.txt`) ?? []).filter((l) => /^\d/.test(l) && Number(l.split(" ")[0]) >= t0 && Number(l.split(" ")[0]) <= t1);

   401	    statusCounts: Object.fromEntries([0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6].map((k) => [k, hal.filter((h) => Number(h[0]) === k).length]).filter(([, n]) => n)),
   402	    maxApC: Math.max(...hal.map((h) => Number(h[1])).filter((x) => !Number.isNaN(x))),
   403	    allOnline: log.every((f) => f[1] === "0-7"),

   412	export const mtkSource = { commit: "${commit}", dir: "${DIR}" };

   414	// Rows are the processor that read the prompt, columns the one that wrote the reply, both in
   415	// CPU, GPU, NPU order, over the turns every cell run completed. A null cell did not fit in memory
   416	// (fits: 5, the pairings that hand over between the NPU and llama.cpp). seconds: those turns' summed time
   417	// (prompt, first token, handoff, reply); handoffSeconds: the state copies between processors in it.
   418	// stalls: CPU decode steps over 500 ms; longestStepS: the cell's longest decode step. startApC: the median AP temperature at each turn's start;
   419	// cappedTurns: turns that started with a CPU ceiling lowered; hotTurns: thermal status 3 or more.
   420	export type MtkCell = {
   421	  seconds: number;
   422	  prefillTokS: number;
   423	  decodeTokS: number;
   424	  handoffSeconds: number;
   425	  decodeShare: number;
   426	  stalls: number;
   427	  longestStepS: number;
   428	  startApC: number;
   429	  cappedTurns: number;
   430	  hotTurns: number;
   431	  gate: string;
   432	  gateWaitS: number | null;
   433	  order: number;
   434	};

   438	  quant: string;

   440	  unit: string;

   453	  snapdragon: { run: string; npuOverCpu: number; turns: number } | null;
   454	  // NPU-prefilled turns whose first reply token matched CPU only's, of how many, and how many distinct first tokens the NPU gave.
   455	  npuFirstToken: { same: number; of: number; tokens: number } | null;
   456	  turnKeys: string[];
   457	  // Heat while the model's compared turns ran, from its device's logger: samples, the share with every CPU
   458	  // limit at its top, each CPU group's lowest limit (GHz), the share of Android heat-status readings at
   459	  // critical (4) or above, the hottest AP reading, all cores online, CPU decode steps and those over 500 ms.
   460	  heat: { samples: number; topShare: number; lowestGhz: number[]; criticalShare: number; maxApC: number | null; allOnline: boolean; cpuSteps: number; slowSteps: number } | null;
   461	};

    13	  href,
    14	  "Two flagship chips of one generation run the same way: the same models, files, turns, order and cooling gate, on a Galaxy Tab S10+ (Dimensity 9300+) and a Galaxy S24 Ultra (Snapdragon 8 Gen 3), each processor pairing for reading the prompt and writing the reply.",
    15	);
    16	
    17	const range = (xs: number[], d = 1) =>
    18	  Math.min(...xs).toFixed(d) === Math.max(...xs).toFixed(d) ? Math.min(...xs).toFixed(d) : `${Math.min(...xs).toFixed(d)} to ${Math.max(...xs).toFixed(d)}`;
    19	const andList = (xs: string[]) => (xs.length < 2 ? xs.join("") : `${xs.slice(0, -1).join(", ")} and ${xs.at(-1)}`);
    20	const short = (m: { name: string; quant: string }) => `${m.name}${m.quant.startsWith("Q8_0") ? " Q8_0" : ""}`;
    21	const tok = (x: number) => (x >= 100 ? grouped(x) : x.toFixed(1));
    22	const known = (xs: (number | null)[]) => xs.filter((x): x is number => x !== null);
    23	
    24	// The two devices, named the same way everywhere on the page.
    25	const MTK = "Dimensity 9300+";
    26	const SD = "Snapdragon 8 Gen 3";
    27	const deviceOf = (m: MtkModel) => (m.npuExport.startsWith("Hexagon") ? SD : MTK);
    28	
    29	// The comparison: every model both devices ran in full, each with the tablet's rows on exactly the turns
    30	// the S24 read (the generator rebuilds the tablet's on a subset when the S24 read one).
    31	const both = sdModels.flatMap((s, i) => {
    32	  const t = sdTwins[i];
    33	  return t ? [{ s, t }] : [];
    34	});
    35	const ratioOf = (m: MtkModel, phase: "prefill" | "decode", a: number, b: number) => rateRatio(m[phase], a, b);
    36	const over = (pick: (x: { s: MtkModel; t: MtkModel }) => MtkModel, phase: "prefill" | "decode", a: number, b: number) => known(both.map((x) => ratioOf(pick(x), phase, a, b)));
    37	const sdNpuPre = over((x) => x.s, "prefill", 2, 0);
    38	const mtkNpuPre = over((x) => x.t, "prefill", 2, 0);
    39	const sdNpuDec = over((x) => x.s, "decode", 2, 0);
    40	const mtkNpuDec = over((x) => x.t, "decode", 2, 0);
    41	const sdGpuPre = over((x) => x.s, "prefill", 1, 0);
    42	const mtkGpuPre = over((x) => x.t, "prefill", 1, 0);
    43	const fastest = (m: MtkModel) => ranked(m)[0];
    44	// Whether every model's fastest pairing on both devices wrote its reply on the CPU.
    45	const cpuWrites = both.every(({ s, t }) => fastest(s).decode === "CPU" && fastest(t).decode === "CPU");
    46	// Each model's fastest pairing on the tablet against the S24's, in percent of the S24's time.
    47	const tabletLead = both.map(({ s, t }) => 100 * (1 - fastest(t).cell.seconds / fastest(s).cell.seconds));
    48	const modelsNamed = andList(both.map(({ s }) => short(s)));
    49	const subsetModels = both.filter(({ s }) => s.convs < 9);
    50	const sdMissing = ["Qwen3 0.6B", "Llama 3.2 1B", "Gemma 3 1B"].filter((n) => !both.some(({ s }) => s.name === n));
    51	const heatOf = (m: MtkModel) => m.heat!;
    52	
    53	// One bar of a Duel between the NPU (a) and the CPU (b): which was faster at one phase, and by how much.
    54	const duelRow = (label: string, m: MtkModel, phase: "prefill" | "decode"): DuelRow => {
    55	  const npu = m[phase][2];
    56	  const cpu = m[phase][0]!;
    57	  if (!npu) return { label, who: "b", blocked: true, shown: "NPU refused to load", detail: `CPU ${tok(cpu.tokS)} tok/s` };
    58	  const npuLeads = npu.tokS > cpu.tokS;
    59	  const ratio = npuLeads ? npu.tokS / cpu.tokS : cpu.tokS / npu.tokS;
    60	  return { label, who: npuLeads ? "a" : "b", ratio, shown: `${ratio.toFixed(1)}×`, detail: `NPU ${tok(npu.tokS)}, CPU ${tok(cpu.tokS)} tok/s` };
    61	};
    62	const duelMax = (ms: MtkModel[]) =>
    63	  Math.max(
    64	    2,
    65	    ...ms.flatMap((m) =>
    66	      (["prefill", "decode"] as const).flatMap((ph) => (m[ph][2] && m[ph][0] ? [Math.max(m[ph][2]!.tokS / m[ph][0]!.tokS, m[ph][0]!.tokS / m[ph][2]!.tokS)] : [])),
    67	    ),
    68	  );
    69	
    70	// What only the tablet ran, kept apart from the comparison.
    71	const tabletOnly = mtkModels.filter((m) => !both.some(({ s }) => s.name === m.name && s.quant === m.quant));
    72	const noHandoff = andList([...new Set(mtkModels.filter((m) => m.fits === 5).map((m) => m.name))]);
    73	const noNpu = andList([...new Set(mtkModels.filter((m) => m.fits === 4).map((m) => m.name))]);
    74	const crit = mtkHot.filter((u) => u.probes.hot6?.status === 4 && u.probes.cool6);
    75	
    76	export default function DimensitySnapdragon() {
    77	  const snapdragonPost = (
    78	    <Go href="/research/prefill-decode/" className={link}>
    79	      the Snapdragon 8 Elite Gen 5 post
    80	    </Go>
    81	  );
    82	  const stallPost = (
    83	    <Go href="/research/decode-stall/" className={link}>
    84	      why CPU decode stalls when a phone throttles
    85	    </Go>
    86	  );
    87	  return (
    88	    <Post
    89	      href={href}
    90	      sources={[
    91	        { label: "The tablet's run notes", href: mtkLinks.notes },
    92	        { label: "Their raw results", href: mtkLinks.results },
    93	        { label: "The benchmarks", href: mtkLinks.bench },
    94	      ]}
    95	      sourcesNote="Every table and chart is generated from the raw results in the OpenWeights repository, both devices' through the same script and the same checks."
    96	      related={["/research/prefill-decode/", "/research/decode-stall/", "/research/8-elite-dimensity/"]}
    97	    >
    98	      <Section id="findings" title="What we found">
    99	        <Findings
   100	          items={[
   101	            {
   102	              headline: "Reading the prompt, Snapdragon's NPU led and MediaTek's did not",
   103	              line: (
   104	                <>
   105	                  Reading the prompt (prefill) on the NPU, the chip&apos;s AI accelerator, ran at {range(sdNpuPre, 1)} times the CPU&apos;s rate on the {SD} and{" "}
   106	                  {range(mtkNpuPre, 2)} on the {MTK}, over the same turns of {modelsNamed}.
   107	                </>
   108	              ),
   109	            },
   110	            {
   111	              headline: "Writing the reply, both NPUs trailed their CPUs",
   112	              line: (
   113	                <>
   114	                  Writing it a token at a time (decode), the NPU ran at {range(sdNpuDec, 2)} times the CPU&apos;s rate on the {SD} and {range(mtkNpuDec, 2)} on the {MTK}.
   115	                </>
   116	              ),
   117	            },
   118	            {
   119	              headline: cpuWrites ? "So on both, the fastest pairing wrote on the CPU" : "The fastest pairings differed",
   120	              line: (
   121	                <>
   122	                  {andList(
   123	                    both.map(({ s, t }) =>
   124	                      name(fastest(s)) === name(fastest(t))
   125	                        ? `${short(s)}: ${name(fastest(s))} on both`
   126	                        : `${short(s)}: ${name(fastest(s))} on the ${SD}, ${name(fastest(t))} on the ${MTK}`,
   127	                    ),
   128	                  )}
   129	                  .
   130	                </>
   131	              ),
   132	            },
   133	            {
   134	              headline: "The GPUs split",
   135	              line: (
   136	                <>
   137	                  The {SD}&apos;s GPU read prompts at {range(sdGpuPre, 1)} times its CPU&apos;s rate, the {MTK}&apos;s at {range(mtkGpuPre, 2)}.
   138	                </>
   139	              ),
   140	            },
   141	            {
   142	              headline: tabletLead.every((x) => x > 0) ? `The ${MTK} still finished first` : "Neither device finished first every time",
   143	              line: (
   144	                <>
   145	                  Comparing each device&apos;s fastest pairing over the same turns, the {MTK} took {range(tabletLead, 0)}% less time; it has four big CPU cores (Cortex-X4) to the{" "}
   146	                  {SD}&apos;s one.
   147	                </>
   148	              ),
   149	            },
   150	            {
   151	              headline: "The Snapdragon device ran hotter, and neither got stuck",
   152	              line: (
   153	                <>
   154	                  Every CPU clock limit sat at its top{" "}
   155	                  {range(
   156	                    both.map(({ s }) => heatOf(s).topShare),
   157	                    0,
   158	                  )}
   159	                  % of the time on the {SD} and{" "}
   160	                  {range(
   161	                    both.map(({ t }) => heatOf(t).topShare),
   162	                    0,
   163	                  )}
   164	                  % on the {MTK}; {both.reduce((a, { s }) => a + heatOf(s).slowSteps, 0)} and {both.reduce((a, { t }) => a + heatOf(t).slowSteps, 0)} CPU decode steps took over
   165	                  half a second.
   166	                </>
   167	              ),
   168	            },
   169	          ]}
   170	        />
   171	      </Section>
   172	
   173	      <Section id="results" title="The results">
   174	        <Figure
   175	          id="devices"
   176	          title="Two devices of one generation"
   177	          lede="The same core designs in different numbers, and each vendor's own GPU and NPU, as read from the devices themselves."
   178	          note="Core types and top clocks from the devices' CPU registers and clock limits."
   179	        >
   180	          <PhoneSpecs phones={[mtkSpec, sdSpec]} throttle={false} />
   181	        </Figure>
   182	
   183	        <Figure
   184	          id="npu"
   185	          title="The NPU against the CPU, on each device"
   186	          lede="Which of the two was faster, at reading the prompt and at writing the reply, and by how many times. A bar grows toward the faster one."
   187	          note="Tokens per second (tok/s; a token is a word or part of one), pooled over every pairing each processor ran in. The scale is logarithmic, so 2× either way is drawn the same length."
   188	        >
   189	          <Duel
   190	            a="NPU"
   191	            b="CPU"
   192	            max={duelMax(both.flatMap(({ s, t }) => [s, t]))}
   193	            groups={(["prefill", "decode"] as const).map((ph) => ({
   194	              title: ph === "prefill" ? "Reading the prompt (prefill)" : "Writing the reply (decode)",
   195	              rows: both.flatMap(({ s, t }) => [duelRow(`${short(s)}, ${MTK}`, t, ph), duelRow(`${short(s)}, ${SD}`, s, ph)]),
   196	            }))}
   197	          />
   198	        </Figure>
   199	
   200	        <Figure
   201	          id="fastest"
   202	          title="The fastest pairing on each device"
   203	          lede="A pairing is the processor that reads the prompt, then the one that writes the reply. Total time for the same turns on both devices."
   204	          note="NPU → CPU means prefill on the NPU, decode on the CPU. Both read only the turns that fit the 2,048-token window of the tablet's NPU (the most text it can hold)."
   205	        >
   206	          <Scroll label="The fastest pairing on each device">
   207	            <table className="w-full min-w-[44rem] text-sm tabular-nums">
   208	              <thead>
   209	                <tr className="align-bottom">
   210	                  <th className={th}>Model</th>
   211	                  <th className={th}>Device</th>
   212	                  <th className={thNum}>Turns</th>
   213	                  <th className={th}>Fastest</th>
   214	                  <th className={thNum}>Its time</th>
   215	                  <th className={thNum}>CPU only</th>
   216	                  <th className={`${thNum} pr-0`}>Slowest</th>
   217	                </tr>
   218	              </thead>
   219	              <tbody>
   220	                {both.flatMap(({ s, t }) =>
   221	                  [t, s].map((m) => {
   222	                    const r = ranked(m);
   223	                    const top = r[0].cell.seconds;
   224	                    const last = r.at(-1)!;
   225	                    return (
   226	                      <tr key={`${short(m)}${deviceOf(m)}`} className="border-t border-rule align-top">
   227	                        <th scope="row" className="py-3 pr-4 text-left font-medium">
   228	                          {mtkLabel(m)}
   229	                        </th>
   230	                        <td className="py-3 pr-4">{deviceOf(m)}</td>
   231	                        <td className={td}>{m.turns}</td>
   232	                        <td className="py-3 pr-4 font-medium text-blue-deep">{name(r[0])}</td>
   233	                        <td className={td}>{grouped(top)} s</td>
   234	                        <td className={td}>{r[0].prefill === "CPU" && r[0].decode === "CPU" ? "fastest" : slower(m.cells[0][0]!.seconds, top)}</td>
   235	                        <td className="py-3 text-right">
   236	                          {name(last)}, {slower(last.cell.seconds, top)}
   237	                        </td>
   238	                      </tr>
   239	                    );
   240	                  }),
   241	                )}
   242	              </tbody>
   243	            </table>
   244	          </Scroll>
   245	        </Figure>
   246	
   247	        <Figure
   248	          id="first"
   249	          title="Which device finished the same turns first"
   250	          lede="Each device's fastest pairing, in seconds for the same turns. A shorter bar finished sooner."
   251	          note="Each device ran its own fastest pairing; the Dimensity 9300+ has four Cortex-X4 cores and the Snapdragon 8 Gen 3 one."
   252	        >
   253	          <PairBars
   254	            a={MTK}
   255	            b={SD}
   256	            shortA="Dimensity"
   257	            shortB="Snapdragon"
   258	            shared
   259	            rows={both.map(({ s, t }) => ({
   260	              label: short(s),
   261	              detail: `${MTK}: ${name(fastest(t))}; ${SD}: ${name(fastest(s))}`,
   262	              a: { value: fastest(t).cell.seconds, shown: `${grouped(fastest(t).cell.seconds)} s` },
   263	              b: { value: fastest(s).cell.seconds, shown: `${grouped(fastest(s).cell.seconds)} s` },
   264	            }))}
   265	          />
   266	        </Figure>
   267	
   268	        <Figure id="matrices" title="Every pairing, on both devices">
   269	          <MtkMatrices models={both.flatMap(({ s, t }) => [t, s])} device={deviceOf} />
   270	        </Figure>
   271	
   272	        <Figure
   273	          id="heat"
   274	          title="Heat while the models ran"
   275	          lede="Each device's own logger over each model's turns: CPU clock limits every 2 seconds, Android's heat status every 10."
   276	          note={`Android's heat status runs from 0 to 6, and 4 is critical. Lowest CPU limits are per core group, slowest group first. A shell reads clock limits and whether cores are online, not the switches behind them; all eight cores read online in ${
   277	            both.every(({ s, t }) => heatOf(s).allOnline && heatOf(t).allOnline) ? "every sample on both" : "most samples"
   278	          }.`}
   279	        >
   280	          <Scroll label="Heat while the models ran">
   281	            <table className="w-full min-w-[46rem] text-sm tabular-nums">
   282	              <thead>
   283	                <tr className="align-bottom">
   284	                  <th className={th}>Model</th>
   285	                  <th className={th}>Device</th>
   286	                  <th className={thNum}>All CPU limits at top</th>
   287	                  <th className={thNum}>Lowest CPU limits</th>
   288	                  <th className={thNum}>At critical</th>
   289	                  <th className={thNum}>Hottest</th>
   290	                  <th className={`${thNum} pr-0`}>CPU decode steps over half a second</th>
   291	                </tr>
   292	              </thead>
   293	              <tbody>
   294	                {both.flatMap(({ s, t }) =>
   295	                  [t, s].map((m) => {
   296	                    const h = heatOf(m);
   297	                    return (
   298	                      <tr key={`${short(m)}${deviceOf(m)}`} className="border-t border-rule">
   299	                        <th scope="row" className="py-3 pr-4 text-left font-medium">
   300	                          {mtkLabel(m)}
   301	                        </th>
   302	                        <td className="py-3 pr-4">{deviceOf(m)}</td>
   303	                        <td className={td}>{Math.round(h.topShare)}%</td>
   304	                        <td className={td}>{h.lowestGhz.map((g) => g.toFixed(1)).join(" / ")} GHz</td>
   305	                        <td className={td}>{Math.round(h.criticalShare)}%</td>
   306	                        <td className={td}>{h.maxApC === null ? "unread" : `${h.maxApC.toFixed(1)} °C`}</td>
   307	                        <td className="py-3 text-right">
   308	                          {h.slowSteps} of {grouped(h.cpuSteps)}
   309	                        </td>
   310	                      </tr>
   311	                    );
   312	                  }),
   313	                )}
   314	              </tbody>
   315	            </table>
   316	          </Scroll>
   317	        </Figure>
   318	
   319	        <More id="numbers" title="Every number" summary="Each processor's rates on both devices, the caveats, and what only the tablet ran">
   320	          {[MTK, SD].map((d) => (
   321	            <div key={d}>
   322	              <p className="mb-2 text-sm font-medium">{d}</p>
   323	              <MtkRates models={both.map(({ s, t }) => (d === SD ? s : t))} />
   324	            </div>
   325	          ))}
   326	          <Note>
   327	            Rates pooled over the pairings each processor ran in; the fastest is in blue. The {MTK}&apos;s NPU ran MediaTek&apos;s runtime on its own export (16-bit activations,
   328	            8-bit weights, made from the same models); every other processor ran llama.cpp on the same GGUF file (Q4_0, 4-bit), so the tablet&apos;s NPU alone read different
   329	            numbers, and a reply written after a handoff to or from it can part from the same decoder&apos;s own.
   330	          </Note>
   331	          <Note>
   332	            Every Qwen3 reply whose prompt the {MTK}&apos;s NPU read began with <code>&lt;|file_sep|&gt;</code> where every other processor, the {SD}&apos;s NPU included, began
   333	            with <code>&lt;think&gt;</code>; the rest went on alike, and the cause is untested. The {MTK}&apos;s Llama 3.2 1B ran before we found its runtime ignored Llama
   334	            3.2&apos;s RoPE scaling (how the model encodes position): its NPU replies and handoffs used the unscaled table, and its times stand because every pairing did the same
   335	            work.
   336	          </Note>
   337	          <div>
   338	            <p className="mb-2 text-sm font-medium">Ran on the {MTK} only, not compared</p>
   339	            <Scroll label="Ran on the tablet only">
   340	              <table className="w-full min-w-[40rem] text-sm tabular-nums">
   341	                <thead>
   342	                  <tr className="align-bottom">
   343	                    <th className={th}>Model</th>
   344	                    <th className={thNum}>Pairings</th>
   345	                    <th className={thNum}>Turns</th>
   346	                    <th className={th}>Fastest</th>
   347	                    <th className={thNum}>NPU against CPU, prefill</th>
   348	                    <th className={`${thNum} pr-0`}>NPU against CPU, decode</th>
   349	                  </tr>
   350	                </thead>
   351	                <tbody>
   352	                  {tabletOnly.map((m) => {
   353	                    const p = rateRatio(m.prefill, 2, 0);
   354	                    const d = rateRatio(m.decode, 2, 0);
   355	                    return (
   356	                      <tr key={short(m)} className="border-t border-rule">
   357	                        <th scope="row" className="py-3 pr-4 text-left font-medium">
   358	                          {mtkLabel(m)}
   359	                        </th>
   360	                        <td className={td}>{m.fits}</td>
   361	                        <td className={td}>{m.turns}</td>
   362	                        <td className="py-3 pr-4">{name(fastest(m))}</td>
   363	                        <td className={td}>{p === null ? "refused" : `${p.toFixed(2)}×`}</td>
   364	                        <td className="py-3 text-right">{d === null ? "refused" : `${d.toFixed(2)}×`}</td>
   365	                      </tr>
   366	                    );
   367	                  })}
   368	                </tbody>
   369	              </table>
   370	            </Scroll>
   371	            <Note>
   372	              With Qwen3 1.7B&apos;s NPU copy loaded, {(mtkMemory.leftMb / 1000).toFixed(1)} GB was free, short of the {(mtkMemory.neededMb / 1000).toFixed(1)} GB a CPU or GPU copy
   373	              needs including our safety margin (the tablet restarted twice when memory ran out), so {noHandoff} ran without the four handoff pairings, and the guard refused{" "}
   374	              {noNpu}&apos;s NPU copy before loading.
   375	              {crit.length > 0 &&
   376	                ` Heated on purpose to Android's critical status, the tablet's 6-thread CPU decode ran ${range(
   377	                  crit.map((u) => 100 * (1 - u.probes.hot6!.decodeTokS / u.probes.cool6!.decodeTokS)),
   378	                  0,
   379	                )}% below its cool rate (2 threads: ${range(
   380	                  crit.flatMap((u) => (u.probes.hot2 && u.probes.cool2 ? [100 * (1 - u.probes.hot2.decodeTokS / u.probes.cool2.decodeTokS)] : [])),
   381	                  0,
   382	                )}%). The S24 was not heated this way.`}{" "}
   383	              The Snapdragon 8 Elite Gen 5 in {snapdragonPost} paused six of eight cores in its hard throttle and its CPU decode nearly stopped: {stallPost}.
   384	            </Note>
   385	          </div>
   386	        </More>
   387	      </Section>
   388	
   389	      <Section id="method" title="How we ran it">
   390	        <Rows>
   391	          <Row label="Devices">
   392	            A Galaxy Tab S10+ ({MTK}) and a Galaxy S24 Ultra ({SD}), both 12 GB, Android 16, on Firebase Device Streaming with a shell and no root, {mtkRun.dates}. Each model ran
   393	            every pairing on one unit of each, in one session, after a throwaway warm-up.
   394	          </Row>
   395	          <Row label="Runtimes">
   396	            CPU and GPU on both: llama.cpp {mtkRun.llamacpp}, 6 CPU threads, built for the same CPU target (Armv8.7-A with dot-product and 8-bit matrix instructions); the GPU
   397	            through Vulkan on the {MTK} and OpenCL on the {SD}. NPU: MediaTek&apos;s LlamaRuntime (ExecuTorch {mtkRun.executorch}, NeuroPilot) on the {MTK}, llama.cpp&apos;s
   398	            Hexagon backend on the {SD}: each vendor&apos;s working path for these models.
   399	          </Row>
   400	          <Row label="Models">{modelsNamed}, the same Q4_0 files (checksums match) on both.</Row>
   401	          <Row label="Turns">
   402	            The same conversations and turns on both, up to the tablet NPU&apos;s 2,048-token window; each reply decodes as many tokens as the dataset&apos;s, from the
   403	            dataset&apos;s history, so every pairing does the same work.
   404	            {subsetModels.length > 0 && ` ${andList(subsetModels.map(({ s }) => short(s)))} read two conversations of each dataset on both, to fit the S24's session.`}
   405	          </Row>
   406	          <Row label="Order and gate">
   407	            Each model&apos;s pairings in one seeded order; before each, up to {mtkRun.gate.maxS / 60} minutes for {mtkRun.gate.limitC} °C or less, every CPU clock limit at its top
   408	            and Android&apos;s heat status at 0. The same logger on both.
   409	          </Row>
   410	        </Rows>
   411	      </Section>
   412	
   413	      <Section id="limits" title="Limits">
   414	        <Rows>
   415	          <Row label="Runtimes and form">
   416	            The NPUs ran different software, and a tablet sheds heat a phone cannot, so this compares two devices as shipped, not two chips in isolation.
   417	          </Row>
   418	          <Row label="Processes and order">
   419	            The S24 ran every pairing in one process, in the seeded order. The tablet split some into processes (NPU with CPU, NPU with GPU, CPU with GPU) to fit its memory, which
   420	            changed their order and what else was loaded; small gaps are within one order on one unit.
   421	          </Row>
   422	          <Row label="Units and runs">
   423	            One unit per model on each device, one run per pairing; units of one model ran at different heat under the same load.

   388	  const t0 = Math.min(...mrows.map((r) => r.wall_ms)) / 1000;
   389	  const t1 = Math.max(...mrows.map((r) => r.wall_ms + r.turn_ms)) / 1000;
   390	  const raw = (lines(`${session}/mtklog-${session}.txt`) ?? []).filter((l) => /^\d/.test(l) && Number(l.split(" ")[0]) >= t0 && Number(l.split(" ")[0]) <= t1);
   391	  const log = raw.map((l) => l.split("|")[0].trim().split(/\s+/));
   392	  if (!log.length) continue;
   393	  const ceil = log.map((f) => [Number(f[4]), Number(f[6]), Number(f[8])]);
   394	  const hal = raw.filter((l) => l.includes("|")).map((l) => l.split("|")[1].trim().split(/\s+/));
   395	  const steps = mrows.filter((r) => r.decode === "cpu").flatMap((r) => r.step_ms);
   396	  mtkSessions.push({
   397	    session: session.slice(1),
   398	    samples: log.length,
   399	    topShare: round((100 * ceil.filter((c) => c.every((x, i) => x >= TOP[i])).length) / ceil.length, 1),
   400	    lowestGhz: [0, 1, 2].map((i) => Math.min(...ceil.map((c) => c[i])) / 1e6),
   401	    statusCounts: Object.fromEntries([0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6].map((k) => [k, hal.filter((h) => Number(h[0]) === k).length]).filter(([, n]) => n)),
   402	    maxApC: Math.max(...hal.map((h) => Number(h[1])).filter((x) => !Number.isNaN(x))),
   403	    allOnline: log.every((f) => f[1] === "0-7"),
   404	    cpuSteps: steps.length,
   405	    slowSteps: steps.filter((x) => x > STALL_MS).length,
   406	  });
   407	}
   408	
   409	const ts = `// Generated by scripts/build-prefill-decode-mtk-data.mjs from OpenWeights at ${commit.slice(0, 12)}
   410	// (${DIR}). Do not edit by hand: re-run the script.
   411	
   412	export const mtkSource = { commit: "${commit}", dir: "${DIR}" };
   413	
   414	// Rows are the processor that read the prompt, columns the one that wrote the reply, both in
   415	// CPU, GPU, NPU order, over the turns every cell run completed. A null cell did not fit in memory
   416	// (fits: 5, the pairings that hand over between the NPU and llama.cpp). seconds: those turns' summed time
   417	// (prompt, first token, handoff, reply); handoffSeconds: the state copies between processors in it.
   418	// stalls: CPU decode steps over 500 ms; longestStepS: the cell's longest decode step. startApC: the median AP temperature at each turn's start;
   419	// cappedTurns: turns that started with a CPU ceiling lowered; hotTurns: thermal status 3 or more.
   420	export type MtkCell = {
   421	  seconds: number;
   422	  prefillTokS: number;
   423	  decodeTokS: number;
   424	  handoffSeconds: number;
   425	  decodeShare: number;
   426	  stalls: number;
   427	  longestStepS: number;
   428	  startApC: number;
   429	  cappedTurns: number;
   430	  hotTurns: number;
   431	  gate: string;
   432	  gateWaitS: number | null;
   433	  order: number;
   434	};
   435	
   436	export type MtkModel = {
   437	  name: string;
   438	  quant: string;
   439	  npuExport: string;
   440	  unit: string;

    88	## Handoff quality in the matrix (session 4, Llama 3.2 1B, 2-prompt calibration)
    89	
    90	- llama.cpp -> NPU (cpu:npu, gpu:npu) replies are coherent and on topic up to pos 821.
    91	- NPU -> llama.cpp GPU (npu:gpu) replies are fine at pos 399 and at pos 521 in one conversation but
    92	  word salad at pos 513 and 821 in two others: content dependent, not a fixed position, so drift
    93	  between an A16W8 NPU cache and a Q4_0 llama.cpp reader is the leading explanation, not an index
    94	  bug. Timings are unaffected (every cell does the same fixed work); quality is reported per cell.
    95	- Leading-token agreement with the same-decoder cell (analyze_mtk.py) is confounded by the first
    96	  token, which comes from the prefill processor; read replies, and the long-history cosine check
    97	  (`--check-handoff --check-conv C --check-turn T`, cosine by 256-position range), instead.
    98	- A nine-prompt calibration export of Llama 1B (`...-cal9`) is building to test whether
    99	  calibration is the cause (qwen.py/llama.py/gemma.py calibrate on short prompts only).

   123	## The NPU replies past a few hundred tokens: Llama 3 RoPE scaling (found and fixed, session 4)
   124	
   125	- NPU -> llama.cpp cells (npu:cpu, npu:gpu) wrote word salad at pos 513 and 821, fine at turn 0.
   126	- Long-history check (`s4/check-long-{single,incremental}.jsonl`): turn-by-turn and single
   127	  prefill identical (no misalignment); V cosine flat; K cosine falls with position, 0.999 at 0-255
   128	  to 0.91 past 1,280, in every layer.
   129	- Cause: MediaTek's rotary table (llm_helper/rotary_embedding.cpp) uses the plain frequencies;
   130	  Llama 3.1/3.2 train with llama3 scaling (factor 32, low 1, high 4, original 8192), which llama.cpp
   131	  applies. The NPU model was self-consistent but rotated its keys with the wrong frequencies.
   132	- Fix: patch_bench.py gives the table llama3 scaling when runner.txt names it (rope_llama3_*).
   133	  After it: K cosine 0.993-1.000 at every position range (`s4/check-long-l3.jsonl`), and npu:cpu at
   134	  pos 821 writes the same reply as cpu:cpu (`s4/l3-quality.jsonl`).
   135	- Timings are unaffected (the table's values do not change the compute); the session-4 Llama
   136	  timings stand, its NPU replies and handoffs were computed with the unscaled table.
   137	- The owner's published MediaTek Llama exports and any runtime built from MediaTek's stock runner
   138	  have the same drift; worth checking in ExecuServe and the app.
   139

   168	## Codex review, round three (gpt-6-astra, 2026-11-16 03:30 UTC)
   169	
   170	Scope: patch_bench.py (llama3 RoPE, Dual LUT), split_queue.py + chain.sh admission, place.sh,
   171	fetch_export.sh, analyze_mtk.py. No defect in the llama3 formula (matches transformers' three
   172	branches and llama.cpp's rope_freqs; both float and int16 templates covered; float32 emulation
   173	over 0-2047 differs by ~2.3e-4), the Dual LUT (local table unscaled, as Gemma 3), the `wait $bp`
   174	exit code, or place.sh's stat parsing. Fixed:
   175	- chain.sh admitted per line: a refused model's later processes still ran, and an admitted model
   176	  could be cut short by a later check. Now a model is admitted or refused once, at its first line,
   177	  after its GGUF arrived; a line without a numeric estimate is refused under DEADLINE; a trailing
   178	  field and a final line without a newline are handled. Tested locally with stubs.
   179	- fetch_export.sh matched `*Llama-3.*` for the RoPE lines (Llama 3.1's factor is 8, not 32). Now
   180	  read from Hugging Face at the export's source_revision; any non-llama3 rope_scaling refused.
   181	- analyze_mtk.py: one unit per model enforced, PARTIAL label below nine cells, empty intersection
   182	  handled, conventional median for agreement (Llama npu:cpu 6.5, was reported as the upper value).
   183	- patch_bench.py is not re-runnable on a patched tree (overlapping replacements); the docstring now
   184	  says so; the build script restores pristine files first, so no build was affected.
   185	Not changed: place.sh samples last CPU every 2 s, so it can show crowding, not prove a pause during
   186	a shorter stall; agreement is a warning signal, not a handoff verdict (cosine checks are).
   187	Sessions 4-6 ran the old chain.sh; their queues had one model per session or deadline skips of
   188	whole models, so none ran partially by that bug (s5's Gemma/LFM lines were all skipped).
   189	
   190	## Memory: from Qwen3 1.7B up, the NPU and a llama.cpp copy do not fit together (session 8, unit c8340578)
   191	
   192	Probe on the idle unit before its chain (s8/probe-s8.jsonl, log-s8probe.txt, log-s8mem.txt):
   193	- Qwen3 1.7B A16W4 2k NPU export (4 chunks 1.1 GB + fp32 embedding 1.24 GB): MemAvailable 8.1 GB
   194	  -> 1.46 to 1.69 GB once loaded. VmallocUsed 0.37 -> 4.16 GB (+3.8 GB kernel), the process's own
   195	  RSS 0.71 GB (anon 0.66, file 0.05: the embedding is not resident), the rest (~1.9 GB) neither
   196	  vmalloc nor anon: most likely NeuroPilot's DMA buffers. Qwen3 0.6B A16W8 took 4.3 GB.
   197	- The GPU (and the CPU) copy of the 1.7B Q4_0 needs 2,232 MB by the guard (1.2 x GGUF + 1,024 MB,
   198	  the margin set after session 3's two out-of-memory kernel panics): refused. Without the margin
   199	  ~100 MB would remain, the condition that panicked the tablet.
   200	- So for 1.7B and up the four handoff pairings (cpu:npu, npu:cpu, gpu:npu, npu:gpu) cannot run on
   201	  this 12 GB tablet; each model runs the five that fit: npu:npu alone, and cpu:cpu, gpu:gpu,
   202	  cpu:gpu, gpu:cpu in a llama.cpp process. Qwen3 1.7B Q8_0 uses the same NPU export, so its npu:npu
   203	  repeats Q4_0's on the same unit (a repeatability check), queued last.
   204	- NPU decode, Qwen3 1.7B A16W4, first two turns of WildChat-1M-18, cool unit: 5.98 and 6.01 tok/s;
   205	  prefill 73.5 tok/s (63 tokens) and 134 tok/s (457 tokens).

   310	  decode step passed 53 ms: no stall. On SM8850 the hard throttle paused cores 2-7 and 6-thread
   311	  decode fell to 0.24 tok/s. Here the throttle is the clock ceiling alone.

   314	  started from the Mac (scratchpad trace7.sh) because its hot.sh was already running.
   315	
   316	## Qwen3's first token after NPU prefill is always 151664 (<|file_sep|>), never <think> (151667)
   317	
   318	- Every turn whose prompt the NPU read: Qwen3 0.6B (A16W8) 0/69 match the CPU's first token, Qwen3
   319	  1.7B (A16W4) 0/25; always 151664 where CPU and GPU give 151667, and the reply goes on as after
   320	  <think> ("\n", "Okay", ...). A peer session (cold/warm study) saw the same through the app's own
   321	  disaggregated bridge on a Tab S10+ with the 1.7B export: not this bench's code.
   322	- Probably not a wrong logits row: the other models' first tokens after NPU prefill match the CPU's
   323	  in 71% to 84% of turns (Llama 3.2 1B 51/72 = 70.8%, Gemma 3 1B 54/69 = 78.3%, LFM2.5 1.2B 57/69 =
   324	  82.6%, LFM2.5 2.6B 32/38 = 84.2% before its run finished), missing with ordinary different words;
   325	  GPU prefill matches 95.7% to 100% (LFM2.5 1.2B 66/69). A Qwen-specific export or row problem is not
   326	  ruled out by the other models working (corrected in Codex round four).
   327	- Leading explanation, untested: the 16-bit output, calibrated on two short alpaca prompts, clips
   328	  <think>'s large logit at the top of its range together with <|file_sep|>'s, and argmax returns the
   329	  lower index of the tie. Test: dump the prefill logits' top five on the NPU (needs a bench flag and
   330	  a short session). Timing unaffected (every pairing decodes the same number of steps). Applies to
   331	  execupack's Qwen3 MediaTek exports: tell the owner.

   375	## Codex review, round four (gpt-6-astra, 2026-11-16 ~06:10 UTC): results, analysis and page
   376	
   377	Held publication on three points, all fixed:
   378	- The site generator published a model once each cell had one turn (Qwen3 4B appeared with 5 turns
   379	  while running). It now needs a done row after every cell's start, rejects duplicate turns, unequal
   380	  work and an empty intersection, keeps a heat-probe pair only with three matching turns and a
   381	  logged heat-off, and cuts each session's logger to its matrix turns.
   382	- The page said the RoPE fix came before the Llama runs: Llama 3.2 1B ran before it (times stand,
   383	  NPU replies and handoffs used the unscaled table); Llama 3.2 3B ran after.
   384	- The throttle finding merged two units: only 317808dd reached critical (6-thread decode -2.9%);
   385	  d6761b43 reached status 2 (-4.1%); "no core paused" claimed more than a shell sees. Reworded.
   386	Also: the Snapdragon NPU/CPU prefill ratio is now recomputed on the tablet's own turns from the
   387	Snapdragon runs' raw rows (9.2 to 14.6x on matched turns; was 11 to 16x on all 33); process grouping
   388	is a stated limit (NPU decode in GPU -> NPU ran -24% against CPU -> NPU on LFM2.5 1.2B; Qwen3 0.6B's
   389	NPU -> CPU won on decode, 48.2 against 40.5 tok/s, not prefill); memory claims are refusals under the
   390	guard's margin, not proofs; the Qwen3 first-token defect is disclosed on the page as untested; the
   391	analyzer separates lowered ceilings from thermal status; dates, unit reuse and Q8_0 corrected.
   392

     1	# Snapdragon 8 Gen 3 (Galaxy S24 Ultra) prefill/decode matrix, unrooted, 2026-11-16
     2	
     3	Why: the MediaTek tablet (Dimensity 9300+, late 2023) read prompts on its NPU at 0.5 to 0.9x its
     4	CPU, where the Snapdragon 8 Elite Gen 5 reference phone (late 2025) read them 9 to 15x on matched
     5	turns. The 8 Gen 3 is the Dimensity 9300+'s generation, with the same core types (Cortex-X4 and
     6	A720; 1 X4 + 5 A720 + 2 A520 here, 4 X4 + 4 A720 there), so it separates "when" from "who". Same
     7	three models as the tablet's cleanest rows, same turns (2,048-token window rule), same seeded orders.
     8	
     9	## Build and package (VM mtk-build, ~/sdw)
    10	
    11	llama.cpp b10549 (b2e5e9b), ghcr.io/snapdragon-toolchain/arm64-android:v0.7 (NDK r29, Hexagon SDK
    12	6.6.0.0), preset arm64-android-snapdragon-release (CPU -march=armv8.7a+fp16+dotprod+i8mm, as the
    13	tablet's build; OpenCL; Hexagon skels v73/v75/v79/v81). The first configure failed with every
    14	try_compile failing ("CMAKE_CXX_COMPILER not set"); rerun with --debug-trycompile it configured and
    15	built. pd_bench_sd from tools/snapdragon/pd_bench_sd.cpp (pd_bench.cpp ported: unrooted heat via
    16	dumpsys thermalservice + cpufreq + kgsl, the tablet's gate, --window/--pad-batch, MemAvailable
    17	check, the tablet's field names). Binary sha256 prefix fb8524aa5e738bbb, libs 0e6b212c7f149d5d.
    18	
    19	## Device (session hir29d7405xqa, unit ccc0f521)
    20	
    21	SM-S928B (international S24 Ultra), ro.soc.model SM8650, board pineapple, Android 16, MemTotal
    22	11,351,852 kB. CPU policies 0 (2.27 GHz), 2 (3.15), 5 (2.96), 7 (3.40). Adreno 750, max_gpuclk
    23	1,000 MHz readable, gpuclk not (gpu_cur_mhz serialises as 0 = unreadable; Codex: should be -1).

    25	## Pilot (probe-s24.jsonl): llama.cpp's Hexagon backend runs unrooted on a retail phone

    80	export type MtkPair = { prefill: (typeof processors)[number]; decode: (typeof processors)[number]; cell: MtkCell };
    81	
    82	// The pairings a model ran; a null cell did not fit in the tablet's memory and is not a pairing here.
    83	export const pairs = (m: MtkModel): MtkPair[] => m.cells.flatMap((row, i) => row.flatMap((cell, j) => (cell ? [{ prefill: processors[i], decode: processors[j], cell }] : [])));
    84	
    85	// One processor's pooled rate against another's within a model, when both ran (index: CPU 0, GPU 1, NPU 2).
    86	export const rateRatio = (xs: ({ tokS: number } | null)[], a: number, b: number) => (xs[a] && xs[b] ? xs[a]!.tokS / xs[b]!.tokS : null);

    88	export const ranked = (m: MtkModel) => pairs(m).sort((a, b) => a.cell.seconds - b.cell.seconds);

    17	function Grid({ m, device }: { m: MtkModel; device?: string }) {
    18	  const r = ranked(m);
    19	  const top = r[0].cell.seconds;
    20	  const low = r.at(-1)!.cell.seconds;
    21	  return (
    22	    <div className="min-w-0">
    23	      <h3 className="display text-xl">{mtkLabel(m)}</h3>
    24	      <p className="mt-1 text-sm text-ink-soft">
    25	        {device ? `${device}, unit` : "Unit"} {m.unit}, {m.turns} turns{m.convs < 9 && ` from ${m.convs} of the 9 conversations`}
    26	        {m.fits < 9 && `, the ${m.fits} pairings that fit`}
    27	      </p>
    28	      <Scroll label={`${m.name} ${m.quant}: total time for each pairing`}>
    29	        <table className="w-full min-w-[19rem] table-fixed border-separate border-spacing-1 text-sm tabular-nums">
    30	          <thead>
    31	            <tr>
    32	              <th className="w-[18%] whitespace-nowrap pb-1 text-left align-bottom text-[0.7rem] font-normal leading-4 text-ink-soft">
    33	                prefill <span aria-hidden>↓</span>
    34	                <br />
    35	                decode <span aria-hidden>→</span>
    36	              </th>
    37	              {processors.map((p) => (
    38	                <th key={p} scope="col" className="pb-1 text-left text-xs font-medium">
    39	                  {p}
    40	                </th>
    41	              ))}
    42	            </tr>
    43	          </thead>
    44	          <tbody>
    45	            {m.cells.map((row, i) => (
    46	              <tr key={processors[i]}>
    47	                <th scope="row" className="text-left text-xs font-medium">
    48	                  {processors[i]}
    49	                </th>
    50	                {row.map((c, j) => {
    51	                  if (!c)
    52	                    return (
    53	                      <td key={j} className="rounded-md border border-dashed border-ink/25 px-2 py-2 align-top text-xs text-ink-soft">
    54	                        {m.fits === 4 ? "NPU did not load" : "did not fit in memory"}
    55	                      </td>
    56	                    );
    57	                  const fastest = c.seconds === top;
    58	                  const t = low === top ? 1 : (low - c.seconds) / (low - top);
    59	                  return (
    60	                    <td
    61	                      key={j}
    62	                      className={`relative rounded-md px-2 py-2 align-top ${fastest ? "bg-blue text-white" : ""}`}
    63	                      style={fastest ? undefined : { backgroundColor: `color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-blue) ${Math.round(5 + 22 * t)}%, white)` }}
    64	                    >
    65	                      <span aria-hidden className={`absolute right-1.5 top-1 text-[0.72rem] leading-none ${fastest ? "text-white/70" : "text-ink-soft"}`}>
    66	                        {c.order}
    67	                      </span>
    68	                      <span className="sr-only">, ran {ordinal(c.order)}</span>
    69	                      <span className="block font-medium">{grouped(c.seconds)} s</span>
    70	                      <span className={`block text-xs ${fastest ? "text-white/80" : "text-ink-soft"}`}>{fastest ? "fastest" : slower(c.seconds, top)}</span>
    71	                    </td>
    72	                  );
    73	                })}
    74	              </tr>
    75	            ))}
    76	          </tbody>
    77	        </table>
    78	      </Scroll>
    79	    </div>
    80	  );
    81	}

   100	// Each processor's rate, pooled over the pairings it ran in: prompt tokens over prompt time for
   101	// prefill, reply tokens over decode time for decode.
   102	export function MtkRates({ models = mtkModels }: { models?: MtkModel[] }) {
   103	  const cols: { title: string; vals: (m: MtkModel) => (number | null)[]; d: number }[] = [
   104	    { title: "Prefill, tokens per second", vals: (m) => m.prefill.map((p) => p?.tokS ?? null), d: 0 },
   105	    { title: "Decode, tokens per second", vals: (m) => m.decode.map((p) => p?.tokS ?? null), d: 1 },
   106	  ];
   107	  return (
   108	    <Scroll label="Each processor's prefill and decode rate, model by model">
   109	      <table className="w-full min-w-[40rem] text-sm tabular-nums">
   110	        <thead>
   111	          <tr className="align-bottom">
   112	            <th className={th} rowSpan={2}>
   113	              Model
   114	            </th>
   115	            {cols.map((c) => (
   116	              <th key={c.title} className={`${th} text-center`} colSpan={3}>
   117	                {c.title}
   118	              </th>
   119	            ))}
   120	          </tr>
   121	          <tr>
   122	            {cols.flatMap((c) =>
   123	              processors.map((p) => (
   124	                <th key={`${c.title}${p}`} className={thNum}>
   125	                  {p}
   126	                </th>
   127	              )),
   128	            )}
   129	          </tr>
   130	        </thead>
   131	        <tbody>
   132	          {models.map((m) => (
   133	            <tr key={key(m)} className="border-t border-rule">
   134	              <th scope="row" className="py-3 pr-4 text-left font-medium">
   135	                {mtkLabel(m)}
   136	              </th>
   137	              {cols.flatMap((c) => {
   138	                const v = c.vals(m);
   139	                const hi = Math.max(...v.filter((x) => x !== null));
   140	                return v.map((x, i) => (
   141	                  <td key={`${c.title}${i}`} className={`py-3 pr-4 text-right ${x === hi ? "font-medium text-blue-deep" : ""}`}>
   142	                    {x === null ? "did not load" : grouped(x, c.d)}
   143	                  </td>
   144	                ));
   145	              })}
   146	            </tr>
   147	          ))}
   148	        </tbody>
   149	      </table>
   150	    </Scroll>
   151	  );
   152	}
