hook: PostToolUse Completed
codex
**The timing arithmetic is largely fair, but the post overstates how like-for-like the experiment is.** I found no current mixing of the two devices’ comparison rows. There are material problems with heat sampling and generator safeguards, plus wording that favours Snapdragon’s NPU story and MediaTek’s CPU story in different places.

I checked the raw rows and exercised the generator with in-memory inputs. No files were modified. I treated s24b as unfinished, not as missing evidence that should already exist.

1. **Material — The heat comparison includes time outside the compared turns.**

   [build-prefill-decode-mtk-data.mjs:237](/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-alpha-mobile-inference/4f6e0161-05e2-42cd-beac-ee96f21f3667/scratchpad/codex8/site/build-prefill-decode-mtk-data.mjs:237) takes the earliest turn start and latest turn end, then includes **everything between them**. That includes cooling gates, process gaps and excluded conversations. This contradicts “over each model’s turns” at [page.tsx:275](/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-alpha-mobile-inference/4f6e0161-05e2-42cd-beac-ee96f21f3667/scratchpad/codex8/site/page.tsx:275).

   Recalculation for completed Qwen:

   | Device | Published window: CPU limits at top | Samples within timed turns only |
   |---|---:|---:|
   | Tablet | 2,188 / 2,368 = **92.4%** | 2,161 / 2,329 = **92.8%** |
   | S24 | 533 / 1,613 = **33.0%** | 313 / 1,324 = **23.6%** |

   Thus the current denominator makes the **S24 look less throttled**. Its window contains 289 samples outside timed turns; the tablet’s contains 39.

   This becomes more serious for Gemma’s subset. An in-memory tablet rebuild using the documented first two conversations per dataset produced **17 turns**, but its heat window still contained **52 excluded turn records, representing approximately 995 seconds**. It reported **51.3%** at top; sampling only the selected turn intervals gave **45.5%**. Rebuilding throughput on matching turns does not currently rebuild heat on matching work.

   **No parser asymmetry found:** the header-based parser correctly handles S24’s four policies and the tablet’s three. “Top” uses each device’s own recorded hardware maximum, not its highest observed clock. The older hard-coded three-policy calculation is not used by this comparison.

2. **Material safeguard gap — A completed process can still publish an incomplete model, and cross-device work is not asserted.**

   The checks at [build-prefill-decode-mtk-data.mjs:144](/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-alpha-mobile-inference/4f6e0161-05e2-42cd-beac-ee96f21f3667/scratchpad/codex8/site/build-prefill-decode-mtk-data.mjs:144) require nine represented pairings and subsequent `done` records, but discard failed turns and intersect the remaining turn sets. They do not require successful completion of the intended workload.

   Two read-only, synthetic checks demonstrated the gaps:

   - Marking one Qwen turn failed and setting `done.failures = 1` still published Qwen, silently reduced from **23 to 22 turns**.
   - Adding one prompt token to the same S24 turn in all nine pairings still published **5,920 S24 prompt tokens against 5,919 tablet tokens**. The twin check compares turn keys, not `pos/new_tokens/decode_steps` across devices. See [generator:299](/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-alpha-mobile-inference/4f6e0161-05e2-42cd-beac-ee96f21f3667/scratchpad/codex8/site/build-prefill-decode-mtk-data.mjs:299).

   **Neither defect is present in the completed Qwen data.** Its recorded work matches. The current unfinished s24b Llama is correctly withheld, and s24a’s partial Llama is explicitly excluded. Removing Qwen’s `done` record also correctly withheld it.

3. **Moderate — “Run the same way” is stronger than the disclosed method supports. Gate outcomes are missing from the presentation.**

   [page.tsx:14](/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-alpha-mobile-inference/4f6e0161-05e2-42cd-beac-ee96f21f3667/scratchpad/codex8/site/page.tsx:14) promises the same files and order; [studies-entry.ts:5](/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-alpha-mobile-inference/4f6e0161-05e2-42cd-beac-ee96f21f3667/scratchpad/codex8/site/studies-entry.ts:5) repeats “run the same way.”

   The important differences **are disclosed**, but some are distant from the claims: A16W8 MediaTek export versus Q4_0 in the folded notes at [page.tsx:327](/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-alpha-mobile-inference/4f6e0161-05e2-42cd-beac-ee96f21f3667/scratchpad/codex8/site/page.tsx:327); Vulkan versus OpenCL and MediaTek runtime versus Hexagon at [page.tsx:395](/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-alpha-mobile-inference/4f6e0161-05e2-42cd-beac-ee96f21f3667/scratchpad/codex8/site/page.tsx:395); tablet/phone form, process splits and single-run limits at [page.tsx:415](/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-alpha-mobile-inference/4f6e0161-05e2-42cd-beac-ee96f21f3667/scratchpad/codex8/site/page.tsx:415).

   Actual Qwen order differs substantially: CPU→GPU ran **first on S24, eighth on the tablet**; NPU→CPU ran **fourth versus second**. The memory-driven regrouping is legitimate, but it prevents a controlled same-order claim.

   Gate outcomes also matter:

   - Qwen: tablet **9/9 passed**; S24 **7 passed, 2 timed out**.
   - Llama: tablet **9/9 passed**; all **eight observed s24b gates so far timed out**.
   - Tablet Gemma: **5 passed, 4 timed out**.

   These are in the raw data/notes, but [page.tsx:406](/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-alpha-mobile-inference/4f6e0161-05e2-42cd-beac-ee96f21f3667/scratchpad/codex8/site/page.tsx:406) describes only the gate policy, and the matrices do not show outcomes. This makes unequal starting conditions too easy to overlook.

   The known Llama RoPE defect is disclosed too. Fixed token counts preserve a throughput measurement; they do **not** establish equivalent useful answers.

4. **Moderate — “Finished first” is numerically fair; the core-count explanation is not established.**

   Each device gets its own fastest pairing, with the same selection rule. For Qwen both select NPU→CPU: **192.761 s tablet versus 279.609 s S24**, correctly **31.1% less time**, using S24 time as denominator.

   The juxtaposition with “four big CPU cores … to … one” at [page.tsx:145](/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-alpha-mobile-inference/4f6e0161-05e2-42cd-beac-ee96f21f3667/scratchpad/codex8/site/page.tsx:145), repeated at line 251, invites a causal conclusion the experiment cannot isolate. Thermal conditions, runtime, memory residency and scheduling also differ. The core mix is relevant context, but the text should explicitly avoid attributing the measured lead to it.

   Even within the tablet’s Qwen run, CPU decode ranges from **40.52 to 53.29 tok/s** across pairings. Its NPU→CPU winner reads the prompt *slower* than CPU→CPU (**39.39 versus 22.61 s**) and wins through faster measured CPU decode (**152.66 versus 181.62 s**). One run supports “fastest observed here,” not a reliable pairing prescription.

5. **Moderate wording issue — “Neither got stuck” needs qualification.**

   [page.tsx:151](/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-alpha-mobile-inference/4f6e0161-05e2-42cd-beac-ee96f21f3667/scratchpad/codex8/site/page.tsx:151) is broader than the evidence:

   - S24: **0 / 22,077** CPU decode steps exceeded 500 ms.
   - Tablet: **7 / 22,077**, maximum **1,705.42 ms**.

   Six occurred in one turn and consumed **7.70 seconds** collectively; see [results-s5.jsonl:17](/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-alpha-mobile-inference/4f6e0161-05e2-42cd-beac-ee96f21f3667/scratchpad/codex8/mediatek/s5/results-s5.jsonl:17). Both completed, and there is no sustained decode collapse, but the tablet did exhibit stalls. “Neither suffered a sustained collapse; the tablet had seven slow steps” would match the evidence. The existing caveat that online cores do not prove absence of pauses is sound.

6. **Low — Some cadence and subset labels are inaccurate.**

   The extra S24 post-turn HAL read is real: [pd_bench_sd.cpp:556](/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-alpha-mobile-inference/4f6e0161-05e2-42cd-beac-ee96f21f3667/scratchpad/codex8/pd_bench_sd.cpp:556) versus tablet clocks-only at [pd_bench_mtk.cpp:812](/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-alpha-mobile-inference/4f6e0161-05e2-42cd-beac-ee96f21f3667/scratchpad/codex8/pd_bench_mtk.cpp:812). But “a second … more” at [page.tsx:427](/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-alpha-mobile-inference/4f6e0161-05e2-42cd-beac-ee96f21f3667/scratchpad/codex8/site/page.tsx:427) is unsupported by these rows: mean gaps between successive turns within pairings were approximately **119 ms S24 versus 37 ms tablet**, including all intervening work.

   Logger cadence is nominal: Qwen HAL intervals were **11–13 s on S24**, predominantly **10–11 s on tablet**. Percentages should say “samples,” rather than exactly “time.”

   Also, the restricted Gemma tablet object retains `convs: 9` despite containing six conversations; and S24 policy order `0,2,5,7` is not strictly “slowest group first”—policy 2’s top is **3.1488 GHz**, policy 5’s **2.9568 GHz**.

Where I found no substantive problem:

- **Work and statistics:** completed Qwen has **23 identical recorded turn keys per pairing**, **5,919 new prompt tokens**, **7,359 decode steps**, and **7,382 reply tokens**, on both devices. Pooled rates use the same sum-of-tokens/sum-of-time formula. First-token and decode accounting is symmetric.
- **Cover hook:** mathematically fair. Snapdragon’s pooled NPU/CPU prefill ratio is **1,426.21 / 343.00 = 4.158×**; tablet’s is **153.96 / 308.43 = 0.499×**, displayed **4.2× versus 0.5×**. Both use the same median over shared models—currently just Qwen. This measures acceleration relative to each device’s own CPU, not a direct NPU speed ratio. The cover highlights Snapdragon’s strength, but does not manipulate the statistic.
- **Charts:** shared CPU/GPU/NPU ordering, identical fastest-cell highlighting, symmetric logarithmic NPU/CPU bars and a shared scale for elapsed-time bars. Matrix shading is relative to each device’s own fastest pairing, as labelled. See [MtkViews.tsx:57](/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-alpha-mobile-inference/4f6e0161-05e2-42cd-beac-ee96f21f3667/scratchpad/codex8/site/MtkViews.tsx:57) and [Compare.tsx:44](/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-alpha-mobile-inference/4f6e0161-05e2-42cd-beac-ee96f21f3667/scratchpad/codex8/site/Compare.tsx:44).
- **Tablet-only fold:** clearly labelled and excluded from the current paired findings, charts and cover calculation. I found no leakage through `tabletOnly`, the heat probes or `mtkSessions`; see [page.tsx:31](/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-alpha-mobile-inference/4f6e0161-05e2-42cd-beac-ee96f21f3667/scratchpad/codex8/site/page.tsx:31) and [page.tsx:338](/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-alpha-mobile-inference/4f6e0161-05e2-42cd-beac-ee96f21f3667/scratchpad/codex8/site/page.tsx:338). An unused legacy `snapdragon` field contains older Elite results, but the current comparison does not consume it.

**The timing arithmetic is largely fair, but the post overstates how like-for-like the experiment is.** I found no current mixing of the two devices’ comparison rows. There are material problems with heat sampling and generator safeguards, plus wording that favours Snapdragon’s NPU story and MediaTek’s CPU story in different places.

I checked the raw rows and exercised the generator with in-memory inputs. No files were modified. I treated s24b as unfinished, not as missing evidence that should already exist.

1. **Material — The heat comparison includes time outside the compared turns.**

   [build-prefill-decode-mtk-data.mjs:237](/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-alpha-mobile-inference/4f6e0161-05e2-42cd-beac-ee96f21f3667/scratchpad/codex8/site/build-prefill-decode-mtk-data.mjs:237) takes the earliest turn start and latest turn end, then includes **everything between them**. That includes cooling gates, process gaps and excluded conversations. This contradicts “over each model’s turns” at [page.tsx:275](/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-alpha-mobile-inference/4f6e0161-05e2-42cd-beac-ee96f21f3667/scratchpad/codex8/site/page.tsx:275).

   Recalculation for completed Qwen:

   | Device | Published window: CPU limits at top | Samples within timed turns only |
   |---|---:|---:|
   | Tablet | 2,188 / 2,368 = **92.4%** | 2,161 / 2,329 = **92.8%** |
   | S24 | 533 / 1,613 = **33.0%** | 313 / 1,324 = **23.6%** |

   Thus the current denominator makes the **S24 look less throttled**. Its window contains 289 samples outside timed turns; the tablet’s contains 39.

   This becomes more serious for Gemma’s subset.
